Remarks.&mdash;During April the highest reading of the barometer was 30.12, on the 24th; and the lowest 29.78, on the 30th. The highest temperature was 85% on the 29th; and the lowest 62&deg;, on the 9th. Rain fell on 15 days, measuring inches, against 21 days, measuring 8.65 inches, in the corresponding month of last year. The prevailing winds were from the S.E., and the strongest was recorded on the 27th, averaging 5.7 miles an hour during 24 hours. During May the highest reading of the barometer was 30.08, on the 4th ; and the lowest 29.60, on the 14th. The highest temperature was 89&deg;, on the 14th, 22nd, 25th, 27th, 30th, and 31st; and the lowest 720, on the 1st. Rain fell on 13 days, measuring 61 inches, against 22 days, measuring 15.36 inches, in the corresponding month of last year. The prevailing winds were from the S.E., and the strongest was recorded on the 21st, averaging 9.1 miles an hour during 24 hours. During June the highest reading of the barometer was 30, on the 2nd, 4th, and 5th; and the lowest 29.65, on the 30th. The highest temperature was 910, on the 29th; and the lowest 750, on the 6th, nth, and 12th. Rain fell on 11 days, measuring 15 inches, against 17 days, measuring 15.35 inches, in the corresponding month of last year. The prevailing winds were from the S.E., and the strongest was recorded on the 12th, averaging 8 miles &laquo;n hour during 24 hours.

184 CUSTOMSGAZETTE. APRI-.UNE, NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 190. AMOY DISTRICT. QUEMOY SPIT BUOY-POSITION CHANGED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Quemoy Spit Buoy has been shifted from the position in which it was first placed, as defined in Notice to Mariners No. 173, dated ist September 1883, and that it is now moored just clear of the end of the spit, with Quemoy Pagoda bearing . . . . . . . N. 16 Sa&#39; E., magnetic. Taitan Lighthouse ,,. . . . . . . N. 750 56&#39; W., The shoal patch which is shown on Admiralty Chart No. 1,767 as forming, and on the southern edge of which the buoy was formerly placed, bears from the buoy in its present position S. 450 E., distant a little over i mile. Vessels are warned not to pass between this buoy and Quemoy Island. * SHANoAI, 8th May 1885. No. 191. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DISTRICT. HEN POINT BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Hen Point Beacon Light, which was discontinued on the x6th July 1883, has been re-established in its former position. The illuminating apparatus is fixed dioptric of the sixth order, showing a white light, visible all round. KIANGLOONG &quot; WRECK LIGHT-BOAT. The white light sh6wn from this light-boat has been replaced by a red one. SHANGHAI, 8th May 1885.
